### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion,  
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI -driven supply is 
surpassing USD 2 50bn in 2026 and mak ing up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for fundin g, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end  of the yield curve and  
favour a duration of  3-7 years, where risk -adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 14 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads  have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this  as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe.  The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY d efault rate  
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle.  
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fu ndamentals and stable credit metrics  
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 15 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post -pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could  
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks , as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in  the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.

Global Market Outlook 13 
Fig. 16 EM USD government bonds offer a yield pick-up 
of nearly 3ppt over DM peers 
Bloomberg EM USD Government Bond Index, yield to worst

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM LCY debt faces additional headwinds, especially as 
elevated US real yields and an appreciating USD dampen 
demand from foreign investors and reduce the comparative 
attractiveness of broad -based duration strategies. However, 
real yields remain comparatively supportive in several 
markets, and current valuations signal selective opportunities, 
especially given the restrictive monetary policy stances 
prevalent across many EM economies. Accordingly, EM LCY 
bonds retain their Core holding status, with portfolio emphasis 
placed on country selection and measured duration exposure, 
reflecting the differentiated currency dynamics at play. 
Key risks to both  the USD and LCY segments include a 
potential escalation of the  Middle East conflict , higher oil 
prices, renewed hawkishness from the Fed that could support 
further USD strength and weather-related disruptions, such as 
the effects of El Niño . Climate risks could prolong inflation 
pressures and hinder disinflation in vulnerable EM regions. 
Asia USD bonds – Core holding 
We retain a Neutral allocation to Asia USD bonds, an asset 
class that continues to reward investors with attractive 
nominal yields, favourable supply -demand technicals and 
robust credit fundamentals. Healthy corporate cash flows, low 
leverage and a high share of sovereign-linked issuers lend the 
segment a defensive quality relative to broader credit. 
The resilience has been evident year to date (YTD). Asia USD 
bonds have outperformed  both DM government and DM IG 
corporate bonds  on a total -return basis. We expect this 
relative strength to persist. The combination of carry and 
credit quality (over 60% of issues are sovereign or sovereign-
related) anchors our conviction in the asset class, even as we 
maintain a Neutral stance.

Bond opportunistic views 
Bullish US HY bonds 
We are opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. We expect 
HY corporate earnings and cash flows to remain solid and 
leverage to remain stable. US HY bonds’  shorter-duration 
profile offers lower sensitivity to interest-rate volatility, which 
is an attractive characteristic in an environment of elevated 
term premia. 
Bullish US inflation-protected bonds (TIPS) 
Real yields remain elevated versus historical standards , 
driven by rate hike expectations. Inflation concerns persist as 
energy prices remain subject to upside risks. We believe TIPS 
offer protection against near-term inflation uncertainty.   
Bullish AUD corporate bonds 
The threshold for further RBA hikes has risen, as higher 
energy prices weigh on the growth outlook and complicate the 
policy trade-off. The transmission channel matters too, where 
higher long -end yields could feed through to the property 
market, tightening financial conditions without the RBA having 
to act, casting further doubt on the case for additional hikes. 
Technicals have improved slightly as higher yields slow new 
supply. Supply remains a tail risk, though. The AUD bond

market is much smaller than the USD market, especially at the 
long end of the curve, where demand for duration is thinner 
and the term premium is most sensitive to supply. 
Bullish AAA-rated CLOs 
Concerns about spillovers from p rivate credit are mounting 
amid deteriorating covenant protections in parts of the 
leveraged credit market. However, we believe AAA-rated 
CLOs backed by solid asset portfolios and substantial credit 
enhancements underneath senior tranches should help 
dampen credit risk and volatility. We see a compelling case 
for floating-rate and shorter -duration alternatives as portfolio 
diversifiers amid elevated term premia.  
Close utilities corporate hybrids 
AI capex is set to keep rising, but funding it has become 
harder. Tighter financial conditions now coincide with growing 
competition for capital. Utilities' credit fundamentals remain 
strong. Even so, we close our utilities corporate hybrids idea 
to lock in a gain of 0.5% as a risk-management step.

### Page 14

Global Market Outlook 14

Our view

We downgrade global equities from Overweight to a Core allocation as we expect higher volatility in the near term. We 
expect this volatility to arise as equity market leadership broadens during the transition to a mid-cycle bull market. Equity returns 
will likely moderate from the 15-20% annualised range of the last few years but remain positive. We remain Overweight US and 
AxJ equities, which are expected to deliver the highest earnings growth in 2027 among the major regions. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, given structural AI-driven growth, and upgrade India to Overweight, for diversified 
non-AI growth exposure. South Korea is a Core holding, with attractive valuations but extreme volatility . China is also a Core 
holding, with attractive valuations but lacking a positive catalyst. We are Underweight ASEAN, which has a weaker growth profile. 
We maintain Japan as a Core holding as nominal growth supports broad-based earnings revisions. We also maintain a Core 
allocation to Europe ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings recovery. We remain Underweight UK equities, given the market’s 
limited exposure to growth sectors.

8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap 
Senior Investment Strategist 
Cindy Lam, CFA 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 15

Global Market Outlook 15

Shifting to a mid-cycle bull market  
The last few years have seen strong annual returns of 15-20% 
for global equities , consistent with the early cycle of a bull 
market, driven in large part by a rapid AI buildout. As the AI 
infrastructure buildout is increasingly debt funded  – we 
estimate 40% of AI capex in 2027 would be debt -financed – 
the market is increasingly concerned about the quality of this 
growth and execution risks . W e remain positive on the  
structural AI theme, but we expect investor focus to shift from 
rapid buildout to monetisation . During this transition, we 
expect market leadership to broaden  beyond technology. 
We believe this shift will also be accompanied by higher near-
term volatility and a moderation in global equity returns , 
consistent with a transition to a mid-cycle bull market.  
Fig. 18 Projected 16% earnings growth for global 
equities in 2027 provides a strong tailwind for positive 
equity returns  
2026 and 2027 consensus earnings growth estimates for 
MSCI AC World sectors

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have declined this year, with the 12-
month forward P/E falling to 19x from 22x at the start of the 
year. This reflects higher rates, a valuation headwind, a nd 
worries about the quality of growth, which is increasingly debt-
funded. As market leadership broadens and the US mid-term 
election nears, we expect higher near-term volatility in the US 
market. Nonetheless, US equities have broad-based strength, 
with the ongoing Q3 earnings season point ing to a robust 
economy. In a world of rising geopolitical risks, the US is also 
relatively insulated by its domestically driven growth. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ has the strongest earnings growth  among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key beneficiary of 
the AI capex cycle. AxJ’s P/E valuations have also dropped to 
attractive trough levels, the lowest among major regions. 
Within AxJ, we are Overweight Taiwan, which benefits from 
clear visibility on AI -driven growth and dominance in 
manufacturing advanced AI chips. We also upgrade India to  
Overweight due to its resilient domestically driven earnings, 
providing diversification beyond AI-related growth. India offers 
the highest exposure to defensive sectors (healthcare, 
consumer staples and utilities) within AxJ.  Both Taiwan and 
India offer attractive returns on equity.  
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are positive 
on long-term agreements that improve earnings visibility for 
memory chipmakers. Valuations are attractive  (the lowest in 
AxJ), with supportive corporate governance reforms . Still, 
extreme volatility remains the key overhang. China remains 
a Core holding , given inexpensive valuations , but re-rating 
potential appears delayed amid AI moneti sation struggles, 
lingering regulatory risks and weak domestic demand. We 
remain Underweight ASEAN , as Indonesia’s downgrade 
warning, oil shocks and a well-supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 19 India offers highest AxJ exposure to defensive 
sectors (healthcare, consumer staples and utilities) 
Sector weights for MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal growth is supporting solid broad-based 
earnings revisions , with rising rates supportive of bank 
earnings. Corporate governance reforms also continue to 
boost shareholder value . The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings recovery after three 
stagnant years, and we expect to see a continuation of this 
narrative with Q3 results. Rising rates are supportive of banks’ 
earnings and fiscal expansion is benefiting domestic demand. 
The fiscal situation in France has no easy solution, but we do 
not expect a contagion widening in government bond spreads, 
as the ECB has tools in place to limit contagion.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK, 
making it lag the performance of other markets over the next 
12 months, although UK equities tend to hold up better during 
bouts of volatility.

Global Market Outlook 16

Add MSCI Switzerland and US Healthcare, 
remove Global Power & Electrification, MSCI 
Taiwan, Global Semis and US Comm Services 
We initiate an opportunistic Idea on MSCI Switzerland on 29 
October 2026, having initiated one on US Healthcare on 12 
October 2026. Healthcare and Consumer Staples account for 
over 50% of the MSCI Switzerland  Index’s weight, providing 
exposure to more resilient demand. It also has a sizeable 
financial sector supported by steady wealth management 
growth and well -capitalised insurers , which benefit from 
disciplined underwriting and resilient premium income. 
We closed our opportunistic ideas in Global 
Semiconductors (9 Sep tember 2026 to 2 Oct ober 2026: 
+8.1%), MSCI Taiwan  (9 Jul y 2026 to 2 Oct ober 2026: 
+10.0%), US Communication Services (18 June 2026 to 12 
October 2026: +1.9%), and Global Power & Electrification  
(30 Jul y 2026 to 29 Oct ober 2026: -6.5%). This reflects a 
tactical reduction in exposure to tech -related sectors amid 
higher expected  near-term volatility, rather than a 
deterioration in the fundamental outlook. Over the longer term, 
we remain constructive on these structural themes and may 
revisit these ideas at more attractive entry points.

US Healthcare: We expect US Healthcare to offer resilience 
amid shifting market leadership, with the sector projected to 
grow by 23% in 2027, the second-fastest growth rate among 
MSCI US sectors, underpinned by healthcare innovation. 
Euro area banks: A ‘higher-for-longer’ rate backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and household wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
Global High Dividend:  Provides a higher -income equity 
allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI ACWI 
and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We made two changes on 12 October 2026: we downgraded 
US Comm unication Services to Neutral  to reduce 
concentration in tech -related sectors  and upgraded US 
Healthcare to Overweight, where its defensive exposure 
stands to benefit as investors diversify beyond concentrated 
AI exposure . This month, we downgrade Europe ex -UK 
Industrials to Neutral , as energy -cost shocks and supply -
chain pressures outweigh fiscal support. Elsewhere, US 
Information Technology (IT) remains Overweight. Rising 
funding and execution risks warrant near -term caution, but 
structural AI tailwinds should continue to provide long -term 
growth opportunities. US Materials also stays Overweight  
on sustained demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials. We remain bullish on gold. The Financial sector 
remains Overweight across the US, Europe ex -UK and 
Japan, supported by stable earnings, elevated rates boosting 
net interest income and continued capital market recovery. 
In China, we maintain a broad Overweight stance to preserve 
sector diversification. IT and Communication Services 
remain Overweight, with rapid innovation and investment in 
AI localisation providing tailwinds. Beyond these, we are also 
Overweight Materials  on structurally elevated commodity 
prices, Healthcare on biotech innovation and a robust drug 
pipeline and the Financial sector on easing margin pressure, 
recovering fee income and capital market reform.

Global Market Outlook 17

Our view

• We remain Overweight gold, but trim our three- and 12-month price targets to USD 4,350/oz and 4,700/oz, respectively. 
• We retain our three- and 12-month West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil forecast at USD 100/bbl and USD 80/bbl, respectively.

Gold outlook:  We remain constructive over 6 -12 months, 
viewing the latest pullback as liquidity-driven rather than  a 
deterioration in fundamentals. USD strength is a headwind , 
but gold’s sensitivity to real yields has eased amid continued 
central bank  buying, led by China , and resilient investor 
demand. Speculative positioning near May lows raises scope 
for renewed inflows. High oil prices sustain near-term volatility 
but may eventually lift gold if they constrain global growth.

Oil outlook:  Crude prices remain elevated as shipping 
disruptions drive freight costs to multi-year highs, sustaining a 
geopolitical risk premium . While Middle East exports have 
proven resilient, threats to production infrastructure are 
mounting. Refined products , especially diesel , face the 
greatest supply pressure amid depleting inventories and 
constrained refining capacity. With limited emergency supply 
buffers, oil markets remain vulnerable to prolonged disruption.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 18

Global Market Outlook 18

USD view

USD consolidation with limited upside: Our three-month US Dollar Index (DXY) forecast stands at 101, revised upwards from 
100.20 last month, reflecting a higher expected Fed rate path. With the October Fed rate pause and a potential December rate 
hike largely priced in, further tightening would offer limited additional USD support. Persistent inflation, elevated US government 
bond yields and resilient business activity continue to underpin the USD's interest -rate advantage. However, in September, 
payroll growth slowed sharply to 29,000 (from 133,000 in August), the unemployment rate rose to 4.2% from 4.1%, annual wage 
growth eased to 3.0% from 3.1% and housing starts fell 5.2% m/m. These signs of cooling demand temper expectations of more 
aggressive Fed tightening. We expect DXY to remain rangebound between 101 and 103, with our three-month forecast at 101.00. 
Peaking Fed rates and narrowing rate differentials favour gradual USD weakness: We maintain our 12-month DXY forecast 
at 98.50. We expect a Fed rate hike in December 2026 and another in H1 2027, taking the federal funds target range to a peak 
of 4.25-4.50%, before easing in H2 2027 . Further BoJ normalisation and the ECB's relatively restrictive policy stance should 
progressively narrow the USD's rate advantage. Persistent fiscal and current account deficits increase reliance on foreign capital 
inflows, adding to medium -term pressure  on the USD . Resilient US growth, AI -related capital inflows and elevated US 
government bond yields remain important offsets, supporting DXY’s gradual decline towards 98.50.

Constructive: French fiscal risks and high US bond yields weigh in the near term. 
Germany's multi-year investment programme, resilient exports and a narrowing US 
rate advantage support medium-term EUR recovery.

GBP/USD 1.3374 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound: Higher UK inflation and stronger October PMI support BoE tightening 
expectations. While Healey's plan deferred the biggest fiscal decisions rather than 
resolving them, any future fiscal slippage or gilt market stress would limit GBP gains.

USD/JPY 156.87 157 153 
Rangebound with bearish bias: Further BoJ tightening, lower US rates and 
moderating oil prices support the JPY. However, negative Japanese real rates and 
overseas investment flows could limit the pace of JPY appreciation.

AUD/USD 0.7036 0.72 0.74 
Constructive: Weak employment and a contracting October PMI temper near term 
AUD gains. However, the RBA’s restrictive policy, resilient commodity demand and 
a narrowing US rate advantage support gradual AUD appreciation.

NZD/USD 0.5699 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: Q3 inflation eased to 3.7% y/y from 4.1%, reducing pressure for 
additional RBNZ tightening. Subdued domestic growth and elevated US yields 
favour NZD consolidation towards 0.56 in the near term, before a softer medium-
term USD supports a recovery towards 0.57.

USD/CAD 1.4003 1.40 1.37 
Rangebound with bearish bias: US-Canada trade uncertainty and softer 
Canadian employment limit near-term CAD gains. We expect the Fed to ease in H2 
2027, narrowing the US interest rate advantage and supporting gradual CAD 
appreciation.

USD/CHF 0.8250 0.82 0.80 
Modestly bearish: French fiscal uncertainty supports CHF safe-haven inflows, 
outweighing the wide US-Swiss rate differential, favouring appreciation towards 0.82 
near-term. Fading US rate support from H2 2027 favours further appreciation; zero 
SNB rates and CHF’s funding role are likely to keep CHF appreciation pace gradual.

USD/CNH 6.6690 6.62 6.52 
Modestly bearish: September exports grew 36.3% y/y and the trade surplus 
widened to USD 133.5bn, strengthening exporter USD conversion support. 
Industrial production growth accelerated to 5.5%, while retail sales growth remained 
subdued at 1.1%. PBoC fixing guidance and strong external flows support controlled 
CNH appreciation towards 6.52.

USD/SGD 1.2781 1.28 1.26 
Near-term rangebound; medium-term modestly bearish: The Monetary Authority 
of Singapore maintained its S$NEER appreciation policy unchanged in October, 
following a “very slight” increase in July. Q3 GDP expanded 5.4% y/y, supported by 
technology-related activity. The unchanged appreciating policy band and narrowing 
medium-term US rate advantage favour gradual SGD appreciation towards 1.26.

USD/INR 94.94 – 96 
Modestly constructive: October composite PMI strengthened to 56.7 from 55.9, 
while the RBI raised its repo rate by 25bps to 5.50%, supporting the INR through 
tighter monetary policy. However, September's USD 30.2bn trade deficit, elevated 
oil import costs and external funding needs sustain USD demand, favouring gradual 
INR depreciation towards 96.

USD/MYR 4.0857 – 4.00 
Modestly bearish: September exports grew 54.5% y/y and the trade surplus 
widened to MYR 33.7bn, strengthening export-related FX conversion. Resilient GDP 
growth of 5.3% y/y and a softer medium-term USD support gradual MYR 
appreciation towards 4.00.

USD/KRW 1,360 – 1,450 
Bullish: Strong semiconductor exports support Korea's external surplus, but 
concerns over AI investment returns and concentrated technology equity exposure 
have increased foreign portfolio selling. Overseas investment and the US-South 
Korea yield advantage sustain USD demand, supporting our forecast for USD/KRW 
to rise towards 1,450.

### Page 20

Global Market Outlook 20

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) is at -2 (minimum: -5) in October, signalling a modest Underweight (UW) in global 
equities. Historically, a decline in the model score to -2 has been associated with flat to slightly negative returns. The shift was 
driven by stretched valuations in both global and Asian equities,  weaker fundamentals and deteriorating market technicals. On 
fundamentals, market risk aversion has risen sharply, while PMI new orders have declined over the past three months, pointing 
to a slowdown in the pace of industrial expansion. On technicals, the share of equity markets trading above their 200-day moving 
averages, a key gauge of long-term support, has fallen below 60% for the first time since January  2025. The model rebalances 
in the middle of each month and has generated 11bps of alpha from the equity Underweight initiated on 15 October 2026, lifting 
YTD alpha vs. the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark to 4.0%.  
Our 1 -3-month equity market regime model indicates contained bear market risks across US and global equities . 
Estimated bear market probabilities are 4.2% for the S&P500 and 7.7% for the MSCI AC World, compared with 0.9% and 6.4% 
respectively in the September outlook. Across both models, volatility measures are the main factors keeping probabilities low, 
supported by momentum signals. Together, these outweigh modest upward contributions from macro and cross-market factors, 
leaving little evidence of a bear market regime over the next 1-3 months.  
Our market diversity indicator flags reversal risk in MSCI Europe Real Estate and MSCI China Industrials, which are down 
over 12% and 19% respectively from their August peaks. US equity and USD positioning remain broadly neutral.

Global Market Outlook 21 
Fig. 33 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Modest equity underweight 25% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +1. Fundamentals weakened, with 
macro risk indicator rising above 0.5 threshold and 
the negative three-month change in PMI new orders 
pointing to a slowdown in industrial activity 
expansion 
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded the 
model’s 1 z-score threshold 
• Technical: -1. The percentage of equity markets 
above their 200-day moving averages has fallen 
steeply from above 80% since in August to current 
47% 
Estimated probability rose slightly from 21%  
• Conference board consumer confidence 
worsened to -14.3% y/y 
• PMI new orders to inventory ratio is at 1.14x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x 
• Conference board leading indicators improved 
but remained negative at -0.7% y/y 
• Fed funds rate to natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.7x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World MSCI Europe Real Estate and China Industrials 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. Bear market 
probabilities remain low at 4.2% and 7.7% 
respectively. Volatility measures are the main factors 
keeping these estimates low, supported by 
momentum signals and outweighing modest 
contributions from macro and cross-market factors 
• Market diversity indicator flags reversal 
risk in Europe Real Estate and China 
Industrials. The indices have declined by 
more than 12% and 19%, respectively, from 
their August peaks 
• Investor positioning remains broadly neutral 
across both US equities and the US dollar, 
indicating that investor exposure is not 
especially stretched in either direction 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time 
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses ML to forecast market regimes or future trends 
based on identified market drivers 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach

Requires ensuring liquidity and income 
flows take centre stage 
Securing Tomorrow 
Entails a well-diversified investment and 
protection portfolio with a focus on growth, 
ensuring inflation is accounted for and risks 
are mitigated 
Building for Forever 
Involves greater focus on long-term returns 
given the time horizon of your portfolio can 
be measured in decades, and might also 
include business interests, real estate, 
collectibles or charitable funds

 Managing your wealth through the decades – Today, Tomorrow and Forever

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

Process 
Following a 
holistic 
approach to 
managing  
your wealth 
We follow a rigorous process to ensure your 
needs and objectives are well -understood, and 
your portfolio is well-aligned and manage s to  
deliver on these objectives. 
However, markets constantly evolve and your 
needs change. Hence, we encourage you to 
undertake regular portfolio reviews to ensure your 
portfolio remains aligned to your Today, 
Tomorrow and Forever objectives. This proactive 
approach includes strategic reb alancing based 
on insights from our Chief Investment Office.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
2
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
• The Westpac–DataX Card Tracker
Index* has edged down slightly since
late September, slipping 0.9pts to 160.6
in the week ended October 17. Despite
this, growth in activity continues to track
a robust pace with quarterly momentum

running at close to 2%qtr and annual
growth at 6%yr.
• Some of this reflects revisions. Initial
figures on card activity can see changes
in subsequent weeks due to information
lags. While these are usually small,
the cumulative impact can sometimes
be material. In this case, revisions have
lifted the index in late September by
2.5pts and the 4wk trend growth rate at
the time from 0.2% to 0.6%. We will be
back-testing regular revisions to assess
whether there is a systematic bias that

can be adjusted for in future.
• The revised profile shows quarterly
growth accelerating steadily since
June, posting a 1.9%qtr gain for the
September quarter and tracking a
2.1%qtr pace in the latest week. This is
back near the robust pace seen in late
2024 and early 2025.
• The monthly and weekly reads show
a softer 0.2%mth rise in August was
followed by a solid 0.8%mth gain in
September that has largely carried into
the first two weeks of October (the latest

observation tracking a 0.4%mth pace).
• The detail shows gains are not just
due to higher fuel prices with strong
growth in discretionary services and
international transactions over the last
quarter and steady growth in essential
services as well. To the extent that the
weekly and monthly data is showing
some renewed squeeze from higher fuel
prices, this is showing through most
clearly in discretionary goods, which
have seen activity decline over the last

six weeks, with discretionary services
still posting positive, albeit slower
growth. Quarterly growth in non-fuel
card activity is tracking around 1.4%qtr.
• Official September quarter figures on
inflation and household spending due
in the coming weeks should confirm the
spending momentum and shed more
light on the extent to which it is due to
prices or volumes.

running at close to 2%qtr ...”

Index compares to growth in nominal
consumption as reported in the national
accounts. Growth has shown a clear
acceleration since late June, lifting to
1.9%qtr in the September quarter and

running at 2.1%qtr in the latest week.
Weekly reads in early October have been
the strongest since July 2025.
• Only some of this reflects a price-
driven rise in fuel spend. The unwind of
temporary fuel excise tax cuts in August
and more recent surge in global energy
prices has seen local pump prices push
back above $2.40/litre locally, up from
$1.56/litre in late June.
• Excluding fuel, quarterly growth in card
activity has moderated, but only slightly,

from 1.9%qtr in August to 1.8% in
September with the latest week tracking
a 1.4% quarterly pace. The impact is
more pronounced in monthly terms with
a –0.4%mth decline in August, muted
+0.2% gain in September and subdued
+0.1% monthly pace in the latest week
(c.f. total: –0.4%; +0.2% and +0.1%).
• The 1.9%qtr rise in nominal terms and an
expected price rise of 1.1%qtr point to a
real, inflation-adjusted gain of 0.8%qtr,
in line with Westpac’s forecasts. ABS Q3

CPI figures are due shortly after we go to
press with the Q3 household spending
indicator due Nov 4 and GDP due Dec 2.
Quarterly momentum softening ex fuel, about flat since July

• Chart 4 shows quarterly growth in card

cleaning often result in shifts to historical
estimates. These are typically small but
can be have a material cumulative effect.

revision: for the last week of September, it
lifts the WCTI 2.5pts and the 4wk trend
growth rate from 0.2% to 0.6%, removing
what looked to be a slowing in momentum
at the time. We will be investigating this

issue more closely in coming weeks to see
whether there is a systematic bias that
can be adjusted for in the future.

• Our MHSI and retail card indexes 
are composite measures based on 
transactions in categories that are in 
scope for the ABS monthly household 
spending indicator (MHSI) and retail 
sales estimates (which have been based 
on the MHSI detail since the ABS retail 
survey was discontinued in June 2025). 
• After under-performing the broader 
WCTI in Q2, the MHSI index has moved 
more in line in Q3, posting growth of 
1.6%qtr with the latest week running  at 
2%qtr. The retail card index has been 
more subdued, up 1.1%qtr in Q3, growth 
running at just 0.4%qtr in the latest week.
• The ABS MHSI has been firmer than 
our index since June, mainly reflecting 
strong gains in vehicle sales which are 
not incorporated into our index. Vehicle 
sales appear to have been relatively flat in 
recent months, our estimates suggesting 
the official measure, due Nov 4, should 
show quarterly growth more in line with 
our indicator (Chart 7).
• The more granular detail on the retail 
card index shows non-food retail 
contracting materially over the last four 
weeks, consistent with the weakness 
being observed across discretionary 
goods categories.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 6
• The Westpac Card Tracker draws on
a rich dataset that sheds light on many
aspects of consumer demand. In some
cases, it can also reveal some nuances
about the way spend is captured – in
official statistics, industry measures and
the card data itself. Vehicles provide a

particularly good example of this.
• Vehicle spend has long been a
bellwether for the wider consumer cycle.
As a large discretionary expenditure, it is
more sensitive to interest rate changes,
‘wealth effects’ and sentiment shifts,
particularly around job security. Given
this, ABS measures and industry figures
on vehicle sales have delivered material
upside surprises over the last year.
Vehicle purchases recorded a remarkable
10.3%qtr surge in the June quarter to be

up 7.3%yr with industry figures showing
vehicle sales holding around these levels
in the September quarter. Even allowing
‘extenuating circumstances’ driving
strong demand for EVs, the results seem
to fly in the face of wider interest rate,
cost-of-living and sentiment narratives.
• The card tracker data allows for a deeper
dive. Note that credit and debit cards are
not often used to purchase vehicles with
account transfers or financing typically

used for the main purchase instead.
• However, cards are used for up-front
payments. This shows through in
payment sizes, which are in the hundreds
of dollars (average $750) not the tens of
thousands. As such, card activity should
still give some guide, particularly around
sales volumes.
• Chart 8 shows how transactions have
tracked and highlight another important
point. Most of the data in this space
focuses on new vehicles (for the ABS
this is because used sales are a transfer

between households that ‘cancels out’ for
the sector as a whole). However, industry
estimates show used sales are both more
common and have been tracking a weaker
profile – the card data suggests used sales
are down at least 3%yr (finer estimates
putting the figure closer to 8%yr).
• Age-groups offer another insightful lens.
Activity has been much firmer across
older age groups, particularly in the ‘new
vehicle’ segment where they are more
prominent (accounting for 42% of all

activity) and activity is up on a year ago.
This group has notably stronger balance
sheets and is low exposure to interest
rate rises. In contrast, vehicle spend has
been contracting materially across other
age-groups, particularly used car spend
by the ‘mortgage belt’ 35-54 year olds.
Vehicle-related card activity highlights diverging trends

WESTPAC ECONOMICS 10
About the Westpac card data indicators
The indicators presented in this report are based on the millions of credit and debit card
transactions processed every day. Transactions covering over ten million merchants are
classified into over 700 categories. These are in turn grouped into higher level aggregates

that provide a timely guide to wider economic trends.
The main focus of these indicators is consumer spending. Where possible, we have sought
to exclude ‘non spending’ transactions such as: money transfers; tax payments; loan
repayments; charitable donations; and superannuation contributions.
It should also be noted that these indicators will also be affected by shifts between card and
non-card transactions. This was a significant factor during the COVID–19 pandemic – health

concerns about the use of physical cash leading to significantly higher use of cards vs cash,
particularly where contact–less transactions are available. Transaction flows also include
reversals/refunds which were also a significant phenomenon during the onset of COVID,
especially in areas such as travel.
All transaction data is compiled at a highly aggregated level so that individual customer or
merchant data is never revealed.
Index construction

The key metrics used in this report are indexes of spending–related card activity where the
base of 100 is average activity in 2019. As an example, if transaction flows are 5% above their
average level in 2019, the index read for the period is 105. If flows in a subsequent period are
8% above the average level in 2019, the index read for this period is 108. Growth between the
two periods can be calculated simply as the change between the two index reads, i.e. 2.9%.

All measures are adjusted for regular seasonality. Weekly estimates are generated using
the US Bureau of Labor’s MoveReg weekly seasonal adjustment program. Note that in some
cases, high levels of volatility during the COVID period mean it is not possible to produce
seasonally adjusted estimates for some historical periods.
Also, note that previous versions of this report used different approaches to seasonal
adjustment and measurement more generally. This means Index reads are sometimes not

directly comparable. See the ‘About the Westpac card data indicators’ sections from earlier
reports to more detail.
Classifications
Note that the measures used for card data and in this report do not align completely with
the those used in official ABS statistics, including the ABS household spending indicator,
ABS retail trade survey and ABS estimate of consumer spending published in the national
accounts. There are a range of differences including around both coverage and classification.

As such, the card data should be treated as broadly indicative.
The transaction data is grouped into 26 categories that are then combined into four main as
follows:
Discretionary goods: alcoholic beverages, tobacco, clothing & footwear, furnishings &
household equipment, household appliances, vehicle–related, recreational & cultural goods,
newspaper, books & stationery, and other personal effects.
Discretionary services: transport services (part), recreational & cultural services, gambling,

catering services, accommodation services, other personal care, insurance & financial
services, other services.
Essential goods: food & non alcoholic beverages, medical products, appliances & equipment,
and operation of personal transport equipment.
Essential services: electricity, gas & other fuels, health services, transport services (part),
communications and education.
The report also uses two additional classifications:
MHSI/Retail: based on the extent to which categories cover activity that is in scope for the

ABS monthly household spending indicator and ABS retail trade survey.

Document 428: Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Who finances private credit in the United States_ What the Federal Reserve’s data reveal (2026-10-28).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-28
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

28 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
WHO FINANCES PRIVATE CREDIT IN THE UNITED STATES? WHAT THE FEDERAL RESERVE’S DATA REVEAL
Céline Choulet
SOURCE: FEDERAL RESERVE (ENHANCED FINANCIAL ACCOUNTS), BNP PARIBAS
The private credit market raises many questions, and its lack of transparency is not helping. Official public data remain scarce. The available
estimates come mainly from private sources, whose methodologies vary widely. The Financial Accounts of the Federal Reserve (Fed) now shed

new light on the matter. They reveal that private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt through households (31% of funds’ total financing),
insurers and pension funds (20%), and banks (19%).
Private credit can take several forms. The most common is loans granted by closed-end funds 1 to small and medium-sized, unlisted and highly
indebted companies. These loans are generally variable-rate, with flexible terms based on cash flow rather than a measure of profitability. While

this asset class has inherent vulnerabilities 2 (Bank of France, 2025), it is also particularly well-suited to financing innovative and long-term
projects (such as digital 3, infrastructure, renewable energy and healthcare), especially when combined with an equity stake in the company. Its
slower growth in Europe likely explains, in part, the region’s lag in innovation financing.
Private credit finances 7.1% of US corporate debt…
Private credit has grown rapidly in the United States over the past decade and has established itself as an alternative to bank loans for

a category of borrowers. It now accounts for 17.3% of the total outstanding loans to American non-financial corporations (NFCs) 4 and 7.1%
1 Strictly speaking, a closed-end fund has a fixed term during which investors cannot withdraw their invested capital. However, the sector has diversified since 2020. The Fed distinguishes
between 1) strictly closed-end private funds (private debt funds), 2) business development companies, which may have an indefinite lifespan and allow for occasional redemptions of

shares, and 3) interval and tender offer funds, which offer the possibility of scheduled redemptions.
2 Notably, high credit risk, high leverage, difficulty in valuing loans, opaque credit ratings, occasionally complex credit structures, high concentration within the sector and increasing
liquidity risk.
3 In 2025, nearly half of the direct loans made by private credit funds went to the tech sector (software, data centres and artificial intelligence) (Abbassi et al., 2026).

4 No comparable data exist for the Eurozone. According to France Invest (2026) data, private credit issued by French funds accounted for 4% of annual bank credit flows to NFCs in 2025.
Who finances US private credit funds?

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 28 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
of their debt. Approximately one-third (35%) is managed by strictly closed-end funds, with another third (36%) by semi-open-end funds
that allow occasional early redemptions. The remaining third is held on the balance sheets of non-resident private debt funds (25%) and
securitisation vehicles (4%).
… through banks, insurers and pension funds, and households

In yet another example of the lengthening of the credit intermediation chain in the United States (following that of the mortgage market),
banks are among the main creditors of private credit funds (65% of their debt and 19% of their total financing). The low risk weighting
of loans granted by banks to these vehicles, compared to that of direct bank loans to non-financial corporations, is undoubtedly a factor
(Chernenko et al., 2024). The holdings of insurers and pension funds – consisting mainly of shares in strictly closed-end funds – make

them among the leading investors in private debt funds (30% of issued shares and 20% of total financing). This asset class offers them the
opportunity to diversify their portfolios and attractive returns. The rise of semi-open-end funds has expanded the investor base to include
retail investors (31% of total financing), albeit at the cost of increased liquidity risk, as evidenced by the significant wave of redemptions in
late 2025 and early 2026 5. American households directly hold 70% of the equity issued by semi-open funds and 22% of the shares issued

by strictly closed funds 6.
Abbassi, P ., Aldasoro, I., and Doerr, S. (2026), Financing the digital economy: the role of private credit, BIS Quarterly Review, September
2026
Banque de France (2025), The reshaping of the credit landscape in light of the development of private debt and Its implications for
financial stability, Financial Stability Report, December 2025
Chernenko, S., Ialenti, R. and Scharfstein, D. (2024), Bank capital and the growth of private credit, Working paper

France Invest (2026), Private debt fund activity in France, March 2026
celine.choulet@bnpparibas.com
5 Triggered by concerns regarding private credit funds’ high exposure to the software sector and the quality of the loans granted.
6 This exposure is likely overestimated because, in the Fed’s financial accounts, the household sector includes individuals and non-profit institutions, as well as resident private
equity funds, which form partnerships with private credit funds. In order to align more closely with the European definition, households here include sole proprietors.

《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望

印度 正向 估值及RSI呈現超賣，近期美印雙方經貿合作釋出改善訊號，接下來可開始關注實質政策進展，及相關投資機會(投資與基礎建設概念)

《外資9月轉為小幅賣超，呼應8月轉中立看法》
8月下調東協評等至中立 ，主要考量 6至8月股市累

的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。

《出口支撐成長敘事未變 ，但高通膨也比預期持久 》
東協及印度經濟仍展現韌性，多數國家8月出口維持
雙位數成長且高於長期平均 ，與全球製造業擴張趨
勢相符 ，其中新加坡及馬來西亞受惠 AI強勁資本支
出，8月出口成長年增率逾 5成，顯示外需仍是重要
成長支撐 。強勁的出口與製造活動不僅有助支撐企
業獲利 ，也在一定程度上抵銷高利率對消費與投資
的壓力 。另一方面 ，印尼、菲律賓等先前高通膨國
家雖已進入降溫趨勢 ，但通膨回落速度仍較緩慢 ，
使央行在降息時程上維持審慎 。整體而言 ，目前東
協與印度景氣尚未出現明顯轉弱 ，但高利率環境可
能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。
重點摘要
• 東協及印度出口維持強勁成

長，反映全球製造業景氣仍
具韌性 ，並支撐區域經濟表
現；惟高通膨國家物價降溫
速度偏慢 ，恐使高利率環境
維持更久 ，短期成長仍須仰
賴外需支撐。
資料來源：Bloomberg ， 2026/9/30 ，中國信託商業銀行整理
高通膨國家通膨下降速度緩慢，低通膨的維持穩定
多數國家8月出口仍維持兩位數成長，且高於長期平均
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/9/30，中國信託商業銀行整理

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。
資料來源：GettyImages

生産
7月の鉱工業生産（関西）は前月比－3.9%と4ヵ月ぶりにマイナスとなったが、均してみると持ち直しの動き
がみられる。汎用・生産用・業務用機械が増加した一方、化学、電子部品・デバイスなどが減少した。先行き
は、持ち直しを見込むが、米・イランの対立長期化、中国経済減速などから下振れるリスクがある。

企業景況感（日銀短観）
日銀短観9月調査の業況判断DI（「良い」－「悪い」）は、全産業で＋21ptと6月調査（＋17ｐｔ）から改善。製
造業、非製造業ともに改善した。業種別に見ると、製造業では化学、非鉄金属、はん用・生産用・業務用機
械などが、非製造業は建設、物品賃貸、対個人サービスなどが改善している。先行き（12月）については、
製造業、非製造業ともに悪化が見込まれている。

マインド・景況感
9月の消費者態度指数（季節調整値）は34.7と3ヵ月連続で上昇した。
9月の景気ウォッチャー調査（現状判断DI、季節調整値）は前月差1.9pt低下の44.1となり、横ばいを示す
50を21ヵ月連続で下回った。秋物需要や連休・インバウンドで人出が増加したものの、物価高による消費
マインドの低下から景況感が悪化した。

### Page 2

2026年第二季度，Anthropic每花费1美元的算力/基础设施支出，已能产生2.08美元的营收
数据来源：Anthropic，格林大华期货

### Page 3

SK海力士：2029年的产能已售罄，短缺持续至2030年
10月28日，SK海力士在2026年三季度业绩发布会上交出了一份历史级成绩单——单季营收首次突破100万亿
韩元，营业利润率攀升至80%的历史峰值。
管理层在电话会上明确表示：结合长期供应协议（LTA）锁定量和正在洽谈中的客户需求，“我们DRAM和
HBM未来三年（截至2029年）的产能实际上已全部售罄“。
需求如此旺盛，客户的采购需求量超出我们当前供应能力的两倍以上。
2027年DRAM市场可实现需求增速约20%中段（mid 20%），NAND约10%高段（high 10%）。
管理层同时强调供给端约束的结构性："由于先进制程实施难度不断上升，以及新建产能所需的建设周期，供
不应求的格局预计将持续至2030年“。
全球AI数据中心基础设施市场预计到2030年保持40%的年均增长率。
公司的商业模式正在从传统的“周期性波动”转向基于长期协议的“高可见度确定性增长”。

数据来源：SK海力士，格林大华期货

### Page 4

三星：2028年很难有新增的存储实际产能
10月29日的三季度业绩电话会：供给紧张将延续至2028年。
2027年CapEx增量的相当一部分将用于购置新场地，而非直接用于产能扩张；其次，建设成本上升使得
CapEx数字看上去比实际更大；再者，从新建晶圆厂到实际出片存在较长的建设周期，2027年的投资很难在
2028年形成有效bit产出。
设备价格因供给紧张而上涨，叠加预付款锁产要求，使资本开支数字进一步虚高；设备交期拉长也会制约存
储厂商的产能爬坡节奏。
随着HBM4E和HBM5逐步成为主流产品，其die尺寸将大于上一代，金属制程层数也将显著增加；同时，常规
DRAM向HBM的生产转移将构成物理性供给约束，"这是不可避免的"。
2027年HBM供货量已按目标价格售罄，但仍持续收到大客户的额外需求请求。
三季度，三星已收到大批量QLC SSD追加订单，这一趋势与云端个人AI Agent服务的爆发直接相关。
三星已与10家主要客户完成多年期供应合同签署，另有10家主要客户的谈判正在进行，其中多家已接近最终

阶段。                                                                         数据来源：三星，格林大华期货

### Page 5

闪迪：客户大举追加长单，NBM订单已排至2030年；高带宽闪存（HBF）存储芯片已完成流片
10月30日的2027财年第一季度财报，公司当季实现营收110亿美元，环比增长23%，同比暴增378%；非
GAAP毛利率达到85.1%。
本季度营收增长中“约60%归功于销量增长，40%归功于价格上涨。”基于强劲的订单可见度，闪迪预计第
二财季营收将在122亿至126亿美元之间。公司承诺将100%的盈余现金返还给股东。
随着AI大模型的演进，推理工作负载正在发生结构性变化。更长的提示、更复杂的推理、多模态输入和持久
的用户上下文都会显著增加存储需求。闪存则提供高效保留更广泛上下文所需的容量，避免高昂的重新计算。
从客户那里了解到，他们低估了运行基础设施所需的NAND闪存容量。因此，我们有很多客户再次订购，同
时也在努力拓展新客户。目前不仅有很多老客户在追加订单，新客户也在不断涌入。
闪迪2027年约50%的供应、2028年约三分之二的供应均已被NBM项目预订。
首款高带宽闪存（HBF）存储芯片已完成流片。Goeckeler解释称：“HBF是一种用于推理的新型存储架构，

其读取带宽与HBM相当，但容量更大……旨在通过将NAND级容量更靠近处理器来补充HBM。” 预计2027
年将向客户交付首批样品。
数据来源：闪迪，格林大华期货

### Page 6

微软云Azure本季度收入增速达48%，预计下季度资本开支将超过500亿美元
美东时间10月28日，微软云Azure本季度收入增速达48%，高于市场预期，下季度指引区间进一步提升至
49%至50%的固定汇率增速。
本季度Azure强劲表现背后，效率改善是关键变量。工程团队在GPU和CPU层面持续挖掘效率空间，使微软得
以将原本受供给约束的算力更多分配至Foundry平台。
本季度token定价高于预期，高质量模型带来了更高的每token收益，对季度业绩形成额外贡献。
客户的需求继续超过了我们可用的供应。
企业客户的AI部署正从试验阶段转向生产部署，关注点已从"下一个技术亮点"转向"能否构建稳定系统、掌控
模型权重、管控成本和保障安全"。
企业级业务不是每个季度看一次的东西。这是一个长达十年的过程。
本季度资本支出达452亿美元，部分第三方算力交付延迟至下半年。预计下季度资本开支将超过500亿美元，
全年合计约1700亿美元，下半年将进一步加速。
数据来源：微软，格林大华期货

### Page 7

谷歌电话会：云收入劲增103%，云业务积压订单高达5610亿美元，
10月28日谷歌电话会：云收入劲增103%。
云业务积压订单高达5610亿美元，TPU收入大规模确认将在2027年到来。
就像十年前我们转向移动端一样，我们专注于先打造最佳搜索用户体验，然后再实现变现。
自由现金流转负主要受到TPU库存前置的“时间差”影响，TPU收入将在2027年放量，2027年是收获之年。
由于供给受限，谷歌已在Q3末开始启用第三方计算容量，Q4将进一步加大使用。
TPU系统不再只在谷歌云上提供，现在已可部署在客户自己的数据中心。
2027年资本开支也将“显著增加”。谷歌母公司Alphabet预计全年资本开支1950亿-2050亿美元。
在整个投资周期中，我们预计将产生有吸引力的投入资本回报率，且将显著高于我们的资本成本，并预计将
产生强劲的运营现金流。
数据来源：谷歌，格林大华期货

亚马逊电话会：云收入劲增103%，云业务积压订单高达5610亿美元，
10月29日亚马逊电话会：云服务部门AWS第三季度营收达到459.84亿美元，较上年同期的330.06亿美元增
长约39%，创近五年来最快同比增速，年化收入运行规模达到约1840亿美元。AWS营业利润达到177.73亿美
元，较上年同期的114.34亿美元增长约55%。
AWS加速发展，公司预计2027年和2028年的收入增长率将“超过40%”。
亚马逊的芯片业务“持续保持着每年三位数的增长率”。公司计划在“2027年底”向外部市场推出其
Trainium芯片。
三季度，亚马逊购置物业和设备支出为772.6亿美元，远高于去年同期的351亿美元，远超市场预期的587亿
美元。过去12个月，公司购置物业和设备净额达到2106.6亿美元，同比增长82%。
亚马逊预计今年的支出仍将达到2200亿美元。公司预计“2027年将大幅增加支出”，并且亚马逊“有望获得
可观的财务回报”。
2027年资本开支也将“显著增加”。谷歌母公司Alphabet预计全年资本开支1950亿-2050亿美元。

在整个投资周期中，我们预计将产生有吸引力的投入资本回报率，且将显著高于我们的资本成本，并预计将
产生强劲的运营现金流。                                                                          数据来源：亚马逊，格林大华期货

### Page 14

CoreWeave债券波动不会放缓AI基建增速
数据来源：CoreWeave，格林大华期货
CoreWeave是新兴云服务商，缺乏传统业务现金流支持，主要依靠发债募资，其本次2031年到期的债券收益
率达到15%，发行时债券收益率即高达9.6%，票面利率为9%。
算力租赁价格在继续上升，云服务商的算力服务盈利极为强劲，当前算力基础设施投入一年即可收回成本。
市场担忧的是CoreWeave从算力基础设施投入到获得算力收入，用时过长，现金流能否覆盖利息与资本开支。
划重点：100%的利润  VS 15%的融资成本，哪个更高呢？
CoreWeave公司CEO 表示，称公司拥有逾1042亿美元的订单积压，完全足以支撑所有债务义务。
英伟达CEO黄仁勋出面“灭火”，称CoreWeave财务实力持续改善、融资成本正在下降。一锤定音。
CoreWeave联合创始人Brannin McBee日前接受《金融时报》采访时表示，公司希望获得投资级信用评级，
从而打开更广泛的融资渠道，甚至能够借入“无限”规模的资金池。

退一步讲，即使CoreWeave无法再融资，也只是拱手将快速增长的算力市场让给其它云服务商，而并非意味
着算力市场会出现衰退。单家公司的信用风险和指数级增长的算力需求是两个不同概念。AI基建不会放缓。

### Page 15

10月14日台积电：需求极其强劲，扩产速度前所未有，全球目前有近25项在建项目
数据来源：美光，格林大华期货
2026 Q3业绩电话会：第三季度净利润同比增幅达69%。预计2026年全年的营收将同比增长约44%。
台积电预计第四季度销售额区间为523亿至543亿美元，以中间值533亿美元计算，较市场此前预估的503.5亿
美元高出约6%，显示需求势头依然强劲。
台积电CEO：需求极其强劲，我们没有看到任何放缓迹象。复合年增长率是否处于百分之五十几的高段，并
不是重点，实际增长可能会更高。
台积电确认2026年资本支出区间为600亿至640亿美元，创历史新高。市场对台积电2027年资本支出的一致
预期持续上调，已成为半导体行业景气度的重要前瞻指标。高盛目前预测该数字为850亿美元，瑞银预测为
900亿美元。
台积电正以前所未有的速度扩张，全球目前有近25项在建项目。未来几年台积电将在中国台湾建设13座先进
制程及先进封装工厂，并在海外建设另外5至6座工厂。台积电宣布在台南再增建一座3纳米晶圆厂。

AI加速器强劲的基础需求持续支持AI相关业务。同时，面向智能体AI的CPU需求兴起，以及网络芯片需求持
续增长，也在进一步推动芯片需求。

阿斯麦CEO：设备订单已经排到 2028年，不少企业已经提前洽谈 2029 乃至 2030 年光刻机采购
数据来源：ASML，格林大华期货

### Page 17

美光电话会： 2027年产能已售75%  看不到供需平衡拐点
数据来源：美光，格林大华期货
美光科技公布截至9月3日的2026财年第四财季的业绩报告。数据显示，美光Q4单季营收达到创纪录的542亿
美元，环比增长31%，同比狂飙379%；单季毛利率大幅攀升至87%。
美光目前已签署26项长协锁定约1500亿美元长期订单，2027和2028年存储市场供需将比2026年更为紧缺，
且“看不到供需恢复平衡的尽头”。
结构性的供需增长率差距导致了持续的供应紧张。即使考虑到行业新增的DRAM洁净室空间计划，在强劲的
需求趋势（包括客户不断提出的增量要求）下，我们看不到供需何时能恢复平衡的拐点。
美光2027年超过75%的产出已经获得客户承诺，且当下与客户的大多数谈判已经围绕2028年展开。
在26份SCA及延期协议中，客户的资金承诺已增加至320亿美元，绝大多数为现金存款。客户希望获得2030
年以后的供应保障，我们现在已经签署了延伸至2031年的SCA。
Q4单季，美光产生了惊人的440亿美元运营现金流和332亿美元自由现金流。预计将把100%的超额现金返还

给股东。我们打算从2026年12月9日开始增加资本返还，主要通过股票回购来实现。
公司预计2027财年上半年的CapEx约为250亿美元，下半年将更高，且大部分增量将用于新建晶圆厂（建筑
支出）而非单纯的设备采购。

### Page 18

市场开始押注：面向普通消费者的智能体，可能正在接近大规模普及的转折点
数据来源：Meta，格林大华期货
Muse 的登顶则表明，个人 Agent 正在从产业概念真正走进大众消费市场。这是一个非常强的早期信号，意
味着大家愿意把真实的任务交给 AI 去办，有这个需求。
Muse 在云端给每个用户配了一台独立的虚拟机，里面有一个真正的浏览器，可以安全地存储 Agent 的数据
和已连接服务的凭证。
AI 在这台云端电脑里完成浏览网页、填写表单、调用服务等各类任务。当涉及发送邮件或完成付款等敏感操
作时，Muse 会先请求用户批准。
Agent 已经是共识，产业竞争的焦点是谁能控制 Agent 入口。
Meta 在旧模式还极其赚钱的时候，主动开启了可能颠覆自己旧模式的个人 Agent 业务。Meta 认定一场产
业革命要来，就主动革了自己的命。
Meta 手里握着超 30 亿的日活用户，一旦通过这个基本盘占住了个人 Agent 这个新入口，也就将成为
Agent 时代最强的玩家之一。
超级 App 未必会消失，但它们将逐渐从第一入口，变成 Agent 背后的服务提供者。

### Page 19

GPU云服务掀涨价潮   Nebius再提价20%   客户预订2028年一季度和二季度的算力
数据来源：Nebius，格林大华期货
Nebius宣布于10月1日起全面上调旗下GPU云服务定价，平均涨幅约20%。
此轮提价覆盖H100、H200、B200及B300多款芯片型号，是Nebius今年5月以来的第二轮涨价。
市场将此解读为AI算力需求持续过热、供给仍然紧张的强烈信号。
H100每GPU小时价格从3.85美元升至4.50美元，涨幅约16.9%；
H200每GPU小时价格从4.50美元升至5.40美元，涨幅约 20%；
B200每GPU小时价格从7.15美元升至8.50美元，涨幅约18.9%；
B300每GPU小时价格从7.85美元升至9.50美元，涨幅约 21%；
以B300为例，其价格从5月前的约6.10美元/小时计算，至今累计涨幅已达约56%。
Nebius管理层透露，已有客户预订2028年一季度和二季度的算力，部分订单涉及数万块GPU，公司当前的需
求能见度已超过24个月，较此前约18个月明显拉长。

推算Nebius当前短期客户合同价格可能已达每兆瓦6000万美元以上，未来Vera Rubin架构产品的合同价格或
将突破每兆瓦8000万美元。

### Page 20

OpenAI重磅官宣下一代旗舰模型GPT-6 Astra，欢迎人类进入AGI时代
数据来源：OpenAI，格林大华期货
9月4日，OpenAI重磅官宣下一代旗舰模型GPT-6。它在计算机使用、浏览、软件工程、网络安全、科学和专
业工作领域，全部刷新SOTA。
总裁Greg Brockman宣布：OpenAI已经实现了AGI！Welcome to the AGI era（欢迎来到AGI时代）！在
我看来，世界正式步入AGI时代，也就是AI的综合智力彻底碾压人类了。
计算机使用代际飞跃：Astra可以完成你在电脑上能做的任何事，速度飞快。
在各项基准测试中，OpenAI官博中多次重用了一个词——饱和（saturate），各项天花板级基准彻底被打爆，
榜单已测不出它的上限了，显示GPT-6 Astra在多个领域的绝对统治力。
AI亲自训练AI：这是OpenAI首款在「训练监督」中由先前AI模型发挥重要作用的模型。
OpenAI把Astra称为，世界上最好的计算机使用模型。奥特曼之前反复强调，下一代最让他兴奋的，就是这

一功能。他直言：Astra计算机使用能力，已达人类水平。
GPT-6 Astra已彻底进化为一个坐在电脑前、手握键鼠的超级专家。

### Page 21

SemiAnalysis创始人：算力收益狂飙，两大巨头将垄断全球绝大部分可用算力，推升全球发债利率
数据来源：SemiAnalysis，格林大华期货
SemiAnalysis创始人Dylan Patel预计，2028年OpenAI与Anthropic两大巨头将垄断全球绝大部分可用算力。
今年新增上线算力中约三分之一被 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 瓜分，到 2028 年，两家头部实验室预计将拿下全
球 70%至80% 的增量算力，单家算力规模将超过 50 吉瓦（GW）。更为关键的是性能乘数效应：今年部署
的每一瓦特算力，其搭载的 GB300、TPUv7 或 Trainium3，在每瓦性能上比上一代芯片高出 3 到 5 倍。当
这两家实验室吃下 2028 年全球 70%至80% 的新增算力时，由于这批算力具有最高的能效比，它们实际上垄
断了全世界绝大部分可用的有效计算能力。
算力收益狂飙：算力的基础成本通常在 1000 万至 1500 万美元/兆瓦，而Anthropic 的算力产出已达每兆瓦

5000 万美元，全公司综合收入向 7000 万至 8000 万美元/兆瓦进发，并正向 1 亿美元迈进，这支撑它们以
每兆瓦 2500 万至 5000 万美元的天价向市场扫货。
AI算力的惊人暴利正掀起投资狂潮。2024 至 2029 年全球 AI 相关资本开支预计达 11 万亿美元，其中需通过
信贷市场举债超 5 万亿美元。
由于 AI 数据中心展现出惊人的投资回报率，科技巨头愿意承受 8% 甚至更高的发债成本，对资本的极端争夺
将推高全社会的折现率与发债利率，甚至引发全球借贷成本的系统性上升。
科技巨头的天价举债推高全社会借贷利率，不仅将重创传统资产估值，更将引爆新一轮主权债务危机。

数据来源：WIND，格林大华期货

史纪录97.5%，间接投标人获配80.3%、为历史第四高，显示外资需求异常强劲。5%收益率已具有极
强的吸引力。
数据来源：文华财经，格林大华期货

### Page 25

全球最大资管贝莱德转向 看多短期美债
全球最大资产管理公司贝莱德（BlackRock）宣布对美国国债和股票的核心配置立场作出重大调整，标志着其
坚守近六年的低配美债策略正式终结。
贝莱德投资研究院将短期美国国债上调至超配，将长期美债由低配上调至中性，理由是当前收益率水平已具
吸引力，且市场对美联储加息的预期可能“过度”。
贝莱德策略师：如今收益率已回归我们长期以来预判的高利率水平，希望锁定部分具有吸引力的收益。
贝莱德团队表示，在当前收益率水平下，即便未来12个月内收益率再上行75个基点，美债所产生的利息收入
仍足以抵消价格损失，总回报不至于转负。这一测算为其超配短期美债提供了量化支撑。
贝莱德认为市场当前对美联储加息的定价已走得过远。利率互换数据显示，交易员预计未来12个月内美联储
将至少加息三次，每次25个基点。贝莱德认为，一旦这些押注被修正，短期美债将尤为受益。
贝莱德此次转向，是大型资产管理机构在收益率大幅上行后集体重新审视固定收益配置的缩影。

数据来源：WIND，格林大华期货

### Page 27

纽约联储主席：年内再加息一次后，预计2027年无需进一步加息
10月13日，美联储“三把手”、纽约联储主席威廉姆斯最新表态指出：
1、美国不是一个过热的经济。
2、没有看到除了人工智能和能源之外的广泛价格压力。
3、没有迹象表明劳动力市场正在推动通胀。
4、抑制通胀的力量正在发挥作用。
5、年内再加息一次后，预计2027年无需进一步加息。
6、当前距离美国中性利率“已不远”。AI短期存在供给缺口，长期来看，产能扩张以及生产率提升“会成为
一股反通胀的力量。
7、美国是超过30万亿美元的庞大经济体，货币政策和金融条件收紧正在给其他领域降温。

数据来源：WIND，格林大华期货

### Page 28

全球经济展望：全球处于加息周期，债市是大类资产中的金丝雀
【全球经济展望】
闪迪电话会指出，AI推理需求正重塑NAND市场，客户因“低估所需闪存容量”大举追加长单，NBM订单已排
至2030年。谷歌云第三季度云营收增长103%至307亿美元。微软云Azure本季度收入增速达48%，下季度指引区间
进一步提升至49%至50%的增速。甲骨文预计2028财年下半年自由现金流将转为正值，2029财年自由现金流将超过
300亿美元。SK海力士预计2027年DRAM市场需求将同比增长20%中段，NAND需求增速则在高十位数百分比区间，短
缺将持续至2030年。台积电管理层确认2026年资本支出600至640亿美元，并明确2026至2028年累计支出将大幅超
越过去三年约1010亿美元总量。阿斯麦CEO表示，设备订单已经排到 2028，不少企业已经提前洽谈 2029 乃至
2030 年光刻机采购。贝莱德近六年来首次终结低配美债立场，将短期美债升至超配——核心逻辑是10年期收益
率已从0.9%飙升至5.2%以上，即便未来再涨75个基点仍可正回报。美国企业今年已授权创纪录的1.3万亿美元回

购，执行窗口从10月15日起陆续重开。
全球处于加息周期，债市持续面临巨大压力，债市是大类资产中的金丝雀。

### Page 1

十月 21, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新  硬體  ‧美國

SpaceX 
(SPCX.OQ/SPCX US)

AI 算力租賃貢獻帶動營運由虧轉盈，並可望延續高成長性 
重要訊息 
SpaceX  3Q26 EPS 由虧轉盈並優於市場共識 ，主要來自 AI 業務轉盈；公司
給予 1Q27 營收較 3Q26 成長 140%之展望，盤後股價上漲約 7%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 AI 營收跳升且轉盈，帶動 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元並優於共識。SpaceX 
3Q26 營收季增 86.5%至 145.8 億美元，動能來自 AI 業務營收季增 195%至
75.6 億美元，目前 AI 營收貢獻已超越衛星寬頻。在算力租賃契約陸續開始貢
獻下，3Q26 AI 業務之營業利益自 2Q26 虧損約 12.6 億美元轉盈至 4.3 億美
元，火箭業務亦隨公司增加 執行對外任務而使營業虧損收斂至 700 萬美元，
衛星寬頻業務亦隨用戶數增加而帶動營業利益季增 43%至 23.7 億美元，帶
動公司 EPS 轉盈至 0.35 美元，優於凱基與市場共識預估之 -0.12/0.24 美元。 
加速 AI 建置與 Starship 測試。4Q26 AI 算力租賃業務隨兩家不具名客戶分
別於 10 月與 12 月開始貢獻營收、單月分別貢獻約 11 億美元，預估帶動
4Q26 AI 業務營收季增 82%至 137 億美元。因應 AI 需求， SpaceX 規劃
4Q26 資本支出提升近 1 倍至 350-400 億美元，預計 4Q26 AI 建置量將自
3Q26 之 1.7GW 提升至 2.5-3GW，公司規劃算力約一半用於自用、一半租
賃，並提及新增算力預計在 1 年內回收投資成本。另外，第 15 次 Starship
試射預計於 11 月進行，此次將測試第一級推進器與第二級太空船回收能
力，並規劃 2027 年初測試第二節太空船復用能力，目前以 1H27 達到每週
發射 1 次 Starship、2H27 達到每週發射 2 次的頻率為目標。 
凱基上修 SpaceX  2026/2027 EPS 預估至-0.88/0.80 美元。基於 SpaceX AI
業務獲利改善狀況優於凱基原預期，且隨新客戶陸續貢獻，有助於 AI 營收
與獲利性逐步轉佳，故將 SpaceX 2026/2027 EPS 自原預估之- 1.64/ -0.57 美
元上修至- 0.88/0.80 美元。中長期而言，預期 SpaceX 主要獲利仍將來自衛
星寬頻與 AI 業務貢獻，凱基對長期財務預估大致不變。  
投資建議  
維持「增加持股」評等 ，目標價 227 美元(20 X FY2032  EPS 11.34 美元)。 
投資風險  
內部人持股陸續釋出， SpaceX 在外流通股數增加或為股價短期上行壓力。

股價說明 等級 定義 
 增加持股  (OP) 對個股持正面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現超越凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 持有 (N) 對個股持中性看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現符合凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 降低持股 (U) 對個股持負面看法，預期個股未來十二個月的表現低於凱基證券集團所追蹤的相關市場的總報
酬。 
 未評等 (NR) 凱基證券未對該個股加以評等。  
 受法規限制 
未評等 (R) 
受凱基證券集團內部政策和 /或相關法令限制使凱基證券集團無法進行某些形式的資訊交流，其
中包括提供評等給投資人參考。  
  *總報酬 = (十二個月目標價 -現價)/現價

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

上市前法人說明會重點摘要

耐特 
(8058 TT, NT$102.0, 未評等)

焦點內容 
1. 耐特將於 11 月下旬上市。公司主要生產高性能複合材料，近年積
極轉向半導體載具、 IC Tray 與 BBU 等高階應用材料，高階產品營
收占比已由 2023 年的 3.6%提升至 1H26 的 25.76%。 
2. 受惠 AI 伺服器 BBU 逐步由選配轉為標配，帶動防火隔熱複合材料
需求成長。管理層表示 BBU 為近兩年成長最快的應用之一，未來
將持續配合客戶開發更高功率規格產品。  
3. 1H26 營收 14.2 億元 (2025 全年 24.7 億元)，受惠高階材料營收占
比提升，毛利率較 2025 年提升 2.5個百分點至 29.5%。 
結論 
耐特近年來由傳統工程塑膠轉向高階複合材料，受惠半導體產能持續
擴張及 AI 資料中心帶動 BBU 出貨增加，預期營收有 望持續成長；且隨
高階產品營收占比提升，產品組合改善將進一步帶動獲利加速成長。

營運概況 
 耐特為塑膠複合材料製造業者， 過去主要生產傳統工程塑膠 (電子
電機、運動、醫療) ，2019 年起積極轉型高階材料，並透過與家登
合作開發半導體晶圓載具材料。2021 年開始投入潔淨生產環境建
置及產品開發，2022 年底正式切入半導體供應鏈。  
 公司生產基地主要位 於台灣及中國，其中彰化廠配置  9 條傳產、2 
條半導體及  2 條抗靜電材料產線； 上海廠則配置 5 條傳產及  2 條
半導體產線，並於美國、越南設有銷售據點。  
 半導體業務受惠晶圓廠持續擴產，帶動載具材料需求成長。公司已
成功將材料導入前段製程  FOUP，並逐步拓展至中國成熟製程及日
韓半導體市場；後段製程則聚焦晶片運輸、晶圓切割等抗靜電材料
應用，而 EUV POD 材料已完成送驗，預計明年有進一步進展。  
 IC Tray 主要應用於半導體封裝測試環節，受惠先進封裝與半導體
測試需求增加，對材料尺寸安定性及抗靜電均勻性要求同步提高。
公司自去年起供應日韓客戶 ，並持續將更高規格產品送樣日韓客戶
驗證。 
 BBU 為近兩年成長最快的應用領域之 一，受惠 AI 伺服器建置需求
成長，以及 BBU 逐步由選配轉為標準配置，帶動防火隔熱複合材料
需求增加。  
 公司材料主要供應 BBU 模組廠，持續配合客戶開發更高功率規格產
品，由既有 3kW、5kW 逐步拓展至 8kW、12kW，並同步開發
HVDC 相關材料應用，以擴大 BBU 產品線及未來營收貢獻。

### Page 2

台灣 耐特

十月 29, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

 公司 BBU 材料兼具導熱及防延燒功能的複合材料， 可於高溫下形成
碳化保護層，降低電芯熱失控時火勢向相鄰電芯擴散的風險，並提
高客戶通過相關安全測試的機會。  
 公司近年積極拓展無人機應用，已參與多項開發案，提供具高剛
性、輕量化特性的複合材料，並取得相關航太品質管理認證。  
 未來 3-5 年將持續深化半導體、BBU 等高階材料應用，並拓展無人
機等新興市場，傳統業務則提供穩定現金流。  
 公司於 2025 年加入德鑫半導體聯盟，積極拓展美國亞利桑那州市
場。 
 目前整體產能利用率約 70%，現有廠房仍具新增 2-3 條產線的空
間，預計未來 2-3 年無擴廠需求。  
 主要高階材料包括 PEEK、PEI、PPS、 PPO、PPA 等，其中半導體用
塑料單價最高達每公斤 3,000 元 (對比傳統泛用塑膠每公斤約 40-
80 元)。 
 傳統工程塑膠業務較易受到原物料價格波動影響，公司透過低價備
貨及逐步調漲售價轉嫁成本；高階材料因單價較高、產量較少，受
原物料價格波動影響相對有限。 
 2025-33 年全球抗靜電高分子材料市場 CAGR 約 7%，目前市場主
要由國際大廠 (如 3M、DuPont)  主導，耐特未來有望逐步切入。  
1H26財報 
 1H26 營收 14.21 億元 (對比 2025 年  24.68 億元) ，毛利率  
29.48% (27.02%) 、營業利益率 11.33% (7.94%) ，EPS 1.85 元 
(1.95 元)。 
 1Q26 營收 6.93 億元 (YoY +13.11% 、QoQ +6.20%) ；2Q26 營收 
7.28 億元 (YoY +8.64% 、QoQ +5.04%) 。 
 1H26 高階應用 (先進製程載具、IC Tray 、 BBU) 營收占比達  
25.8%，對比 2025 年為 20.9%，主因半導體及 BBU 材料需求成
長。 
 1H26 產品別營收占比：半導體 19.2% ( 對比 2025 年 15.6%)、電
子電機 33.8% (37.1%) 、汽/ 機車  15.5% (15.1%) 、運動及醫療  
18.7% ( 19.1%) 及其他 12.8% (13.1%) 。其中 BBU 占整體營收比
重由 5.3% 提升至 6.6%。 
 1H26 地區別營收占比：台灣  50% ( 對比 2025 年 47%)、中國  
33% (36%) 、美洲 10% (12%) 及亞洲其他地區 7% (5%)。 
 2025 年配發現金股利 1.3 元，配發率約 67%，未來配發率目標維
持 60-70%。

宏捷科 (8086 TT)

調升 評等  
買進(Buy) 
光通訊業務於 2028 年 迎 來 顯 著 成 長 
⚫ 3Q26 營收略低於預期，毛利率預期受原物料成本呈現下滑  
⚫ 2H26 營運仍可維持高檔水準 ，持 續受惠客戶提升份額  
⚫ GaAs 及 InP PD 有望成為第二成長動能，預期於 2028 年量產  
3Q26 營收 略低於 預期 ，毛利率 預期 受原物料成本 呈現下滑 ：公司 3Q26
營收 12. 62 億(QoQ -2.81% ，YoY+ 12.96 %)，略 低於我們 預期， 預估 毛
利率 為 26.14% %(QoQ -3.84 ppts，YoY -2.05 ppts) ，主要受到上游磊晶及
金價持續處於高檔影響，呈現季下滑 ，預估 3Q26 EPS 為 1.07 元。公
司 3Q26 稼動率為 70%~75% ，下一季度將維持相同稼動率水準，預估
4Q26 營收為 12.63 億(QoQ+0.15% ，YoY+0.85%) ， 此外 ， 公司已於3Q26
與大部分客戶商議產品漲價，產品售價調漲將於 4Q26 生效，毛利率
預期將小幅度回升至 27.83%(QoQ+1.69ppts ，YoY -2.64ppts) 。 
2H26 營運仍 可維持高檔水準 ，持續受惠客戶提升份額 ：展望 手機 PA
因韓系手機持續擴大採用中國供應商，陸系品牌拉貨穩定，且因公司
客戶重心已轉向中高階手機，有效降低記憶體漲價對低階手機市場的
衝擊 。 公司也持續受惠於美系 IDM 廠退出中國市場及中高階手機拉貨
潮，帶動手機 PA 出貨量與 ASP 成長。 Wi-Fi 業務方面， Wi-Fi 7 滲透
率增加帶動 PA 需求，但傳統 Wi-Fi 5/6 部分 市場被矽鍺取代，展望
Router 需求為持平至小幅成長，而手機 Wi-Fi 則因大陸客戶逐漸取代
美系 IDM 廠而成長。 無人機 Wifi PA 因傳輸距離增加及高解析度圖片
回傳需求，客戶持續規格更新及提升需求量，公司現無人機客戶由過
往 1~2 家增加至 4~5 家，成長顯著。  
GaAs 及 InP PD 有望成為第二成長動能， 預期於 2028 年量產 ：公司
在 2026 年 8 月通過 28 億元資本支出 ， 用於擴建無塵室及優化生產線 ，
建立 InP 相關產品製程，預估無塵室於 2027 年底擴建完成。 光通訊業
務方面， 公司 除應用於 Datacom 短距傳輸 VCSE L 外，佈局 GaAs 及
InP PD ，並已與 多位 客戶進行開發與測試 。GaAs PD 進展較為快速，
預估最快將於 2027 年底至 2028 年初進入量產 ，而InP PD 進度較 GaAs 
PD 晚約半年，約於 2028 年年中量產。  
公司 過往 營收有 7-8 成來自手機 PA，在 PA 客戶持續提升份額、無人
機及非射頻相關應用持續顯著成長外，光通訊業務除 VCSEL 外， PD
有望在 2028 年貢獻營收，成為除手機 PA 外下一個主要成長動能。我
們預估 2026 、2027 年 EPS 分別為 5.17 、5.33 元，維持前次報告 本益
比倍數 ，基於 2027 年預估 EPS x 32 倍本益比，目標價為 171 元， 調
升評等至買進 之投資評等建議。 風險因子 ：手機 市場需求不佳 、非射
頻營收不如預期 。

Document 654: 当前AI最紧迫的瓶颈：廉价资金正在枯竭！

Source PDF: all report pdf/当前AI最紧迫的瓶颈：廉价资金正在枯竭！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-31
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

信⼼⽀撑融资，融资⽀撑投资；信⼼⼀旦转弱，资⾦收缩⼜会反过来打击投资和增⻓预
期。
摘要：
过去⼏年， AI 公司轻松筹得数千亿美元，华尔街竞相出资、很少过问，主打⼀个 “ 钱多事少 ” 。如
今，贷款机构和投资者开始变得挑剔起来，条款也愈发苛刻。
本周， AI ⾏业最受追捧的两家头部玩家在资本市场上相继碰壁：⿊⽯告诉投资者， Anthropic 上
市后恐怕只能拿到垃圾级评级； OpenAI 则把新⼀轮融资⽬标从 300 亿美元砍到约 200 亿美元。
两件事指向同⼀个变化：对美国 AI ⾏业来说，廉价资⾦越来越难拿了。
太平洋投资管理公司（ Pimco ）⾸席投资官 Dan Ivascyn 表⽰：
钱还在，但已是买⽅市场
ChatGPT 问世以来， AI 公司⼏乎⽤遍了⻛投、股市、银⾏、债券和私募信贷等所有资⾦来源。
据美国银⾏统计，美国各类公司债市场上，与 AI 相关的发⾏规模 2025 年为 3940 亿美元，今年截
⾄ 10 ⽉中旬已达 6550 亿美元，分析师预计明年将接近 1 万亿美元。

资⾦需求越来越⾼，但出资⽅也变得越来越犹豫了。
出资⽅的顾虑主要有三点：各地对数据中⼼的抵制正在升温，可能拖延⼤型项⽬；超⼤规模云
⼚商以及 Anthropic 、 OpenAI 等初创公司的 AI 利润，未必能像承诺的那样快速兑现；股票和债券
发⾏扎堆，投资者对 AI 的敞⼝已经过重。
新的交易条件体现在市场的每个⻆落。 SpaceX 、 Meta 等⼤型科技公司的债券在⼤幅折价交易；
CoreWeave 、 Nebius 、 Nscale 等靠借债建设算⼒的 AI 云公司，近⼏周实际上已被挡在债券市场
⻔外；就连博通这样的强势公司，也不得不向银⾏提供更优厚的条件才能拿到贷款。
Anthropic 是最新的案例。它计划在 11 ⽉中旬的 IPO 中募资⾄多 1000 亿美元，并在招股书中提
出，到 2045 年在算⼒基础设施上投⼊逾 8000 亿美元。据英国《⾦融时报》报道，⿊⽯近期告诉
投资者，根据主要评级机构的指引， Anthropic 上市后获得 BB 区间或以上评级的可能性更⼤，⽽

⿊⽯此前⼀直预计它将在年底前拿到投资级评级。
按标普和惠誉的体系， BBB- 及以上为投资级， BB+ 及以下为投机级，即俗称的 “ 垃圾级 ” 。许多保
险公司和养⽼基⾦被限制⼤额持有垃圾级债务，评级落在这⼀区间，意味着 Anthropic 能借到钱
的渠道变窄，成本也更⾼。
本周博通与 Anthropic 达成的 630 亿美元芯⽚租赁交易，也是在紧张⽓氛中谈成的。据《华尔街⽇
报》，博通⾸席执⾏官陈福阳（ Hock Tan ）在最后关头亲⾃介⼊谈判，才稳住了银⾏的情绪；交
易中逾 420 亿美元优先级贷款，靠的是博通的 A 档⾼位评级作担保。 Anthropic 还在招股书中把电
⼒供应商的融资约束列为⻛险因素：即便⾃⼰融得到钱，也要担⼼供应商融不到。
“ 向贷款⼈证明这些投资能转化为⾜够收⼊的⻔槛越来越⾼ ... 薄弱环节会最先受冲击。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

OpenAI 的处境类似。据《⾦融时报》报道，它已将融资⽬标削减⾄约 200 亿美元，估值从 1.4 万
亿美元下调⾄ 1.25 万亿美元；《华尔街⽇报》援引知情⼈⼠称， OpenAI 迄今募得的资⾦不到最初
300 亿美元⽬标的⼀半。部分投资者调低了认购额度，还有投资者想等 Anthropic 上市后再做决
定。
虽然信⽤好的头部公司仍然拿得到钱，但市场的耐⼼在变薄。
本周，随着科技公司交出令⼈失望的财报， AI 相关股票和债券遭抛售： Alphabet 周四市值蒸发约
5000 亿美元，创历史最⼤规模；英特尔周五暴跌 29% ；纳斯达克综合指数周五下跌 2.9% ，美股
出现 AI 热潮以来规模最⼤的抛售之⼀。
如果波动持续，融资只会更难。
新云⼚商：最先被关上⻔
压⼒最先落在信⽤最弱的⼀环。
AI 债务市场最活跃的发⾏⼈之⼀，是被称为 “ 新云 ” （ neocloud ）的公司。它们拥有或租赁数据中
⼼，部署 AI 服务器，再把算⼒卖给⼤型科技公司、交易公司和 AI 初创企业。
许多新云⼚商由加密货币挖矿公司转型⽽来，运营 AI 基础设施的经历很短，借贷成本在市场动荡

前就已偏⾼，因此对利率上升最为敏感。
转折发⽣在 9 ⽉。美联储三年来⾸次加息；甲⻣⽂因电⼒供应和审批问题，通知新墨西哥州⼀座
⼤型数据中⼼的开发商，将推迟开始⽀付全额租⾦的时间，各地贷款机构随即重新评估⼿中项
⽬的建设⻛险。
CoreWeave 今年早些时候发⾏的债券票⾯利率为 9.75% ，如今在⼆级市场的收益率已超过
15% 。债券价格下跌，收益率随之上升，说明它借钱的成本在短期内⼤幅攀升。银⾏家近⼏周
提醒，新云⼚商的⾼收益债市场对新发⾏基本已经关闭， 15% 以上的收益率是借款⼈⽆法承受
的。
Nebius ⾃ 2024 年以来已向英伟达等投资者出售约 27 亿美元股票，并发⾏了数⼗亿美元可转债，
但资⾦需求仍未满⾜。它原本考虑发⾏⾸笔⾼收益债，最终作罢，转⽽向客⼾收取预付款。
⾸席执⾏官 Arkady Volozh 上周在柏林表⽰，资产⽀持证券和债券市场等部分资⾦来源 “ 可能暂时
关闭了 ” ，但客⼾预付款等⼯具 “ ⼤概⾜以让我们⾄少建成这 1 吉⽡的产能 ” 。⾸席运营官 Ophir

Nave 则称，资产⽀持融资市场仍然开放。
股权融资也不顺。知情⼈⼠称， Lambda 在 IPO 前最后⼀轮融资中寻求筹集 40 亿美元，但在
Coatue 等投资者决定不参与后，将⽬标估值从 145 亿美元下调⾄ 130 亿美元。
英国新云⼚商 Nscale 原本考虑 11 ⽉上市，如今更倾向于推迟到 12 ⽉。它还在与英伟达等投资者
谈判，为 Anthropic 在西弗吉尼亚州⼀座 450 亿美元的数据中⼼争取资⾦⽀持，市场转向后对⽅要
求更严格的条款，拖慢了它更新招股书的进度。部分银⾏家已告诉 Nscale ，其 IPO 恐难达到此前
设定的 300 亿⾄ 350 亿美元估值。
巨头：评级再⾼，投资者也会 “ 装满 ”
Meta 、亚⻢逊、 Alphabet 和微软处在信⽤⻛险谱系的另⼀端。它们拥有顶级评级，资产负债表
还有⼤量举债空间，问题出在融资规模本⾝。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
今年它们在⾼等级债市场密集发债，并表⽰未来还会继续。为其提供贷款的投资公司、保险公
司和养⽼基⾦已感到额度吃紧，也担⼼持仓过度集中于同⼀⾏业。 “ 现实的问题是，投资者在⼀
个名字、⼀个主题上到底能装多满？ ”Ivascyn 说。
周五，多家超⼤规模云⼚商的债券价格跌⾄接近历史低点，收益率明显⾼于⼀般投资级公司
债。尽管评级顶尖，它们借钱的成本已⾼于普通投资级企业。
谁都想抢在别⼈前⾯。 Meta 计划通过⼀系列新股发⾏募资⾄多 975 亿美元，理由之⼀就是要赶在
竞争对⼿之前出⼿，并在监管⽂件中把其他巨头的融资视为对资本市场承接能⼒的挑战。
“ 每个⼈都想赶在流动性枯竭之前为⾃⼰的建设筹到钱， ”Carnegie Investment Counsel ⾸席投资

官 Ben Connard 说， “ 最终，可能没有⾜够的钱来⽀撑这场建设。融资可能成为让⼀切放缓的最
终瓶颈。 ”
为什么资⾦松紧变得如此关键
AI 建设越来越依赖外部资⾦。据路透社对 LSEG 共识预测数据的分析，微软、 Alphabet 、亚⻢
逊、 Meta 和甲⻣⽂五家公司，到 2027 年的资本开⽀预计将超过其⾃由现⾦流，每新增 1 美元运
营现⾦流，对应约 1.57 美元的新增资本开⽀。⾃⼰赚的钱不够花，缺⼝只能靠发股、发债来补。
巨头们也停不下来。 Cornell Capital Group 的研究报告将此形容为 “ 囚徒困境 ” ：⾃⼰不投⽽对⼿
投了，就可能被淘汰。
投资⼈ Citrini 在接受 Monetary Matters 播客采访时指出，这⼀变化改变了⻛险的性质。 “ 在这场 AI
交易的前三年，情绪驱动的下跌都有⼀个底，因为⼤部分⽀出来⾃超⼤规模云⼚商的运营现⾦
流。 ” 如今，增量⽀出⼏乎都来⾃股权和债务融资， “ 如果市场情绪转向，那些增量资⾦就会枯
竭，因为这不再只是⼀家公司在花钱，⽽是由市场决定的 ” 。

他把这种机制称为 “ 反⾝性 ” ：信⼼⽀撑融资，融资⽀撑投资；信⼼⼀旦转弱，资⾦收缩⼜会反过
来打击投资和增⻓预期。他还指出，当前 AI 收⼊每年增⻓约 25% ⾄ 30% ，资本开⽀增速则接近
60% ⾄ 70% ，两者的缺⼝随每⼀轮业绩指引扩⼤。

Document 672: 日銀の金融政策：審議委員人事と企業金融が鍵に

Source PDF: all report pdf/日銀の金融政策：審議委員人事と企業金融が鍵に.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 20 日(火)

1/3 
日銀の金融政策：審議委員人事と企業金融が鍵に 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・日銀の金融政策を占ううえでは、賃金・物価統計も然ることながら2027年７月の審議委員人
事が重要となる。現在９名で構成される政策委員会のうち最もタカ派に分類される高田委員
と田村委員が、双方とも任期満了になることは重大な変化をもたらす可能性がある。両委員
は、基調的な物価上昇率は既に概ね２％の物価目標の水準にあるという認識を示し、物価の
上振れリスクに警鐘を鳴らし、早期の利上げを主張してきた。なお、９月会合時点において
政策委員会全体としての認識は「基調的な物価上昇率が２％に近づいている」である。

・その２名の後任にリフレ派（極ハト派）が送り込まれるとの見方は根強い。高市政権になっ
てから任命された浅田統一郎委員と佐藤綾野委員は上記２名とは対照的に利上げに慎重な構
えを固持しており、９月会合では反対票を投じた。浅田委員は「足もとの消費者物価（除く
生鮮食品）の上昇率が２％を下回るなか、経済の状況も必ずしも強いとはいえない点を踏ま
えると、金融市場調節方針を据え置くことが望ましい」として、佐藤委員は「経済・物価情
勢が、現時点ではこれまでと比べて大きく加速している状態にあるわけではないと考えられ
るもとで、このタイミングでの利上げは適切ではない」としてそれぞれ反対した。仮に高田
委員と田村委員の後任に２名のハト派委員が就任するならば、利上げに対して潜在的な反対
票が４つ投じられる可能性が高まることから、政策委員会全体としての議論は利上げに慎重
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
になる可能性がある。

・では審議委員人事はいつ動きだすのか。高田委員と田村委員の「政府の人事案」が提示され
たのは、岸田政権下で黒田東彦総裁体制であった2022年３月１日であった。その後、３月25
日までに衆参両院で可決し、７月24日に就任した。筆者の知る限り、審議委員人事に関する
観測記事などは一切なく、市場関係者が人事案を知ったのは３月１日の報道であった。浅田
委員のように2026年２月25日に人事案が提示され、４月１日に就任した事例もあるため、人
事案が提示される具体的時期を探るのは難しいが、2027年３月頃以降は人事案に対する注目
を高めておくべきであろう。

・ちなみに筆者は後任に中立的ないしはタカ派的な人物が選任されると予想している。骨太シ
ョックと呼ばれる円安・長期金利上昇の経験を踏まえ、現政権は金融政策の独立性を脅かす
ような人選は自重するだろう。仮にハト派な人選となれば、その後に続く総裁・副総裁人事
もリフレ色の強いものとなることが市場関係者の間で強く意識され、円安や長期金利上昇と
いった無用な混乱を招く恐れがある。

・次に審議委員人事とは関係のない文脈で利上げが中断・終了するとしたら、どんな要因があ
るか考えてみたい。もちろん、為替および米国景気の減速やそれに応じたＦｅｄの利上げ打
ち止め（ないしは利下げ観測の浮上）が重要であるが、国内要因としては企業金融の引き締
まりが重要であろう。日銀短観の貸出態度判断ＤＩと資金繰り判断ＤＩは現時点で双方とも
安定しているものの、都心の一部において不動産価格に調整がみられるなか、先行きは不動
産向け融資に積極的だった金融機関を中心に融資基準が厳格化する可能性がある。

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
・日銀は９月時点で「金融機関の貸出態度は引き続き積極的」とした。不動産業からみた貸出
態度判断ＤＩと資金繰り判断ＤＩが安定を維持し、銀行貸出残高の伸びも全体としては伸び
ているが、それを牽引しているのは金利敏感業種の代表例である不動産業であることは認識
しておく必要があるだろう。銀行貸出は2026年４－６月期に前年比＋6.5％、そのうち不動産
業向けは同＋8.2％であった。銀行貸出に占める不動産業向け貸出の割合は18％へ達してお
り、今後、累積的な利上げの効果が急速に表面化する可能性は否定できない。また過去、資
金繰り判断ＤＩの悪化に連動する形で倒産件数が増えていることも、一定程度注目しておく
必要があろう。倒産件数はコロナ期の政策支援が減衰していくもとで、増加傾向にあり７―

９月期には2,664件となった。これら指標が悪化を続けるようだと今後の金融政策に一定の影
響を与えよう。

### Page 3

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年10月
◆財部門が生産活動の重石に
足元のユーロ圏景気は 、サービス主導で底堅く推
移。７月のサービス業生産は増勢を維持 。一方、
鉱工業や建設業など財部門の生産活動は低調。
先行きも、生産活動の二極化は続く見通し。旺盛
なＡＩ需要を背景に 、情報通信を中心にサービス
業は好調を維持するものの、エネルギーコストの高止
まりや対中競争力の低下を受けて 、中間財や消費
財の生産は低迷が長引く公算大 。加えて、金利高
による需要減少が、建設活動を抑制する見込み。
◆物価や金利の上昇が内需を下押し
先行きの景気は、インフレ加速や金利上昇を受け
て減速する見通し 。まず、物価高が個人消費を下
押しする公算 。９月の消費者物価は前年比＋
3.8％と、前月から伸びが拡大。資源高が続くことで、
来年初にかけてインフレ率がさらに上昇すると予想。
加えて、金利上昇が住宅投資や設備投資を抑制
する見込み。ＥＣＢは、９月の理事会で政策金利
を0.25％ポイント引き上げ 。インフレ加速が見込ま

れるなか、年内にも追加利上げを行う見通し。
政治面からも金利上昇圧力が増大 。フランスでは、
来年度予算案で財政赤字の対ＧＤＰ比がＥＵ
目標を超えることが示され、長期金利の対独スプレッ
ドが急上昇。スペインでは、住宅価格の高騰対策が
否決され、政府が議会を解散。現時点の世論調査
では、11月の総選挙後に極右勢力が政権入りする
可能性が高く、拡張的な財政運営への警戒が増大。

◆個人消費は先行き減速へ
足元の英国景気は堅調 。８月の小売売上数量
は前月比＋0.5%と、高めの伸び。販売チャネル別
にみると、無店舗販売が持ち直したほか 、百貨店な
ど非専門店の販売も増加。
もっとも、先行きの個人消費は減速する見通し。イ
ンフレの加速が主因。８月の消費者物価は前年比
＋3.1％と、前月から伸びが拡大 。電力・ガス市場
局（Ofgem）は、足元の資源高を踏まえて 、
2027年１～３月期にかけて、エネルギー価格の上
限を段階的に引き上げる見込み 。これにより、インフ
レ率は前年比３％台後半まで上昇すると予想。
◆ＡＩ需要が企業の生産活動を下支え
足元の生産活動は 、業種によって二極化 。ＧＤ
Ｐに占めるシェアが大きいサービス業では、消費者向
けが低調である一方 、企業向けは堅調。旺盛なＡ
Ｉ需要を背景に、ＩＴ関連の情報サービス業などの
事業活動が活発化 。製造業でも 、コンピュータ ・電
子製品・光学機械などＡＩ関連の生産が拡大。

先行きの生産活動は底堅く推移する見通し 。個
人消費の減速を背景に消費者向けサービス業は伸
び悩む一方、ＡＩ関連の企業向けサービス業や製
造業の生産は今後も拡大すると予想。英国の民間
ＡＩ投資額は米国や中国に次ぐ規模であり 、フラン
スやドイツなど大陸欧州諸国よりもＡＩ需要による
生産押し上げ効果が大きくなる公算。

欧州
(株)日本総合研究所 欧州経済展望 2026年10月
◆健康問題と雇用悪化を背景にニートが増加
英国では、ニート（就業せず、教育・職業訓練も
受けていない16～24歳の若者）の数が近年増加
し、2026年４～６月期には約100万人に。その背
景として、次の２点が指摘可能。
第１に、健康問題を抱える若者の増加 。就労に
支障をきたすほどの健康問題を抱えるニートの割合
は、2015年の26％から2025年に44％へ上昇。メ
ンタルヘルスの不調が主要な健康問題に。
第２に、若年層の雇用環境悪化 。16～24歳の
失業率は、足元でコロナ禍のピークを上回る水準に
上昇。企業は採用を抑制しており、小売業や宿泊・
飲食サービス業といった 、従来若年層が多く就労し
ていた産業の欠員は減少。加えて、政府による最低
賃金の引き上げを受けて 、一部の企業では若年層
を採用する意欲が弱まった可能性も。
◆ニートの増加は経済・財政を圧迫
ニートの増加は、英国の経済・財政に悪影響。政
府の委託を受けてニート問題を調査した 「ミルバー

ン・レポート」の中間報告は、ニートが就業していない
ことによるＧＤＰの損失が年間 380億ポンド （約
7.9兆円）、それにより得られなかった税収や追加の
福祉支出などの合計が年間66億ポンド（約1.4兆
円）にのぼると試算。
ニートの数が今後も増加し、無業状態が長期化す
れば、英国の成長力が低下し、税収減や福祉支出
の増加を通じて財政のさらなる悪化につながるリスク 。

欧州経済見通し：ユーロ圏・英国ともに当面は減速
欧州各国の経済・物価見通し
【ユーロ相場見通し】
◆緩やかなユーロ安基調で推移する見通し
９月のユーロドル相場は 、上旬に1.16ドル台前半を中
心に横ばい圏で推移 。ユーロ圏のインフレ率が前月から上
昇した一方、米雇用統計が市場予想を上回り 、ＥＣＢ・
ＦＲＢともに早期利上げ観測が強まったことが背景 。その
後、10月上旬にかけて 、一時1.11ドル台半ばへユーロ
安・ドル高が進行。堅調な米経済指標がドル高圧力となっ
たほか、フランスの財政不安がユーロ安圧力に。
先行きのユーロドル相場は 、緩やかなユーロ安基調で推
移する見通し。当面はフランスの財政不安が重石となるほ
か、ＥＣＢは12月の追加利上げ後 、来春にかけて政策
金利を据え置く一方 、ＦＲＢは追加利上げを実施すると
みられることが背景。もっとも、来年半ば以降は利上げが打
ち止めとなり、横ばい圏で推移する見込み。
＜ユーロ圏＞

◆来年初にかけて減速、その後持ち直し
ユーロ圏の景気は、来年初にかけて減速する見通
し。中東危機による物価や金利の上昇が内需を下
押しする見込み。ただし、南欧諸国を中心とする良
好な雇用環境が個人消費を下支えするほか、世界
的なＡＩ需要の高まりが企業の生産活動を押し上
げることで 、景気の底割れは回避 。ドイツの政府支
出や、復興基金を原資とする各国の公共投資の増
加も景気を下支えすると予想 。インフレ圧力の緩和
を受けて、来春以降、景気は持ち直しへ。
先行きのリスクは 、中東危機の長期化。原油・天
然ガス価格が高止まりする場合 、インフレが想定以
上に長引くことで 、石油・ガスへのエネルギー依存度
が高い南欧諸国を中心に個人消費が大きく落ち込
む恐れ。ＥＣＢが複数回の利上げを行い、ユーロ圏
全体の内需が一段と下押しされる可能性も。
＜英国＞
◆家計部門を中心に、景気は減速へ
英国の景気は、減速する見通し 。世界的なＡＩ
需要の高まりが企業の生産活動や設備投資を下
支えする一方 、物価高や雇用情勢の悪化が個人

消費の重石となる公算。
先行きのリスクは、ユーロ圏と同様、中東危機の長
期化。資源価格が高止まりすれば、景気が大きく下
振れる恐れも。

消費者物価指数（全国・2026 年 9 月） 
～財を中心に物価上昇圧力強まる。＋３％台も視野～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

25 年12 月以来の＋２％台。コアコアの上昇率も明確に拡大 
本日総務省から発表された26年９月の全国消費者物価指数では、生鮮食品を除く総合（ＣＰＩ
コア）が前年比＋2.1％と、8 月の＋1.7％から上昇率が0.4％Pt拡大した。市場予想通りの結果で
ありサプライズはない。＋２％台は 25年 12月以来のこと。また、季節調整済み前月比でも＋
0.5％と、８月の＋0.1％から伸びを大きく高めており、足元では物価上昇ペースの強まりがみら
れる。 
エネルギー以外でも強い動きがみられた。日銀版コア（生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合）
(単位：％）

(出所）総務省統計局「消費者物価指数」
